## Summary

Reviewed 87 file(s) in 40 chunk(s) with 5 reviewer(s). Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud): The changelog is detailed and well-structured, documenting rapid 0.1.x releases (0.1.4-0.1.7 on consecutive days) with many new language and stdlib features. The main problem is the trailing 'Pending Features' section, which contradicts the release notes by listing already-implemented items (PRO/ENDPRO, GOTO, CASE/SWITCH, FITS/HDF5/NetCDF, map projections, 3D visualization) as outstanding. Minor issues include inconsistent date precision, duplicated scientific-I/O sections in 0.1.7, and several advertised functions that are explicitly placeholders. Ollama (kimi-k2.6:cloud): The repository shows mature engineering discipline: CI actions are pinned to SHAs, the post-edit hook carefully sanitises untrusted paths before invoking cargo, and documentation is extensive. The main defects are a hardcoded user path in '.cargo/config.toml' and a shell-scripting oversight where 'cargo check' failures are swallowed because 'set -e' is missing. A logical-vs-physical path mismatch and a locale-dependent regex in the same hook are latent correctness bugs. Ollama (muse-spark-open:cloud): Files are well-commented and security-conscious, but '.cargo/config.toml' hardcodes a developer-specific absolute path that breaks portability and leaks a username, CI workflow inconsistently pins actions despite claiming SHA-pinning, and the post-edit hook has minor robustness gaps around quoting and toolchain assumptions. Ollama (gemma4:cloud): The provided files consist of project configuration, a CI pipeline, a shell hook for automated checks, and a detailed internal developer guide for adding built-in functions. The project demonstrates high maturity in CI/CD practices, including pinned action SHAs and strict boundary testing requirements.

## Issues (716)

### 1. [WARNING] correctness — `docs/ARRAY_FEATURES.md:124`

*Flagged by: Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud), Ollama (kimi-k2.6:cloud), Ollama (muse-spark-open:cloud), Ollama (gemma4:cloud)*

**Description:**
Documents a silent correctness bug: 'arr[-3:-1]' returns an empty array instead of the expected last three elements. Silently producing wrong results (rather than an error) is dangerous for numeric scripting users.

**Suggested Fix:**
```markdown
Normalize negative start/stop bounds against the array length before slicing and add regression tests covering negative start, stop, and step combinations.
```

### 2. [WARNING] security — `docs/DATABASE_QUICK_START.md:89`

*Flagged by: Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud), Ollama (kimi-k2.6:cloud), Ollama (muse-spark-open:cloud), Ollama (gemma4:cloud)*

**Description:**
The PostgreSQL insert example builds SQL by concatenating STRING(value) directly into the statement — the exact anti-pattern the doc warns against at line 274. Beyond modeling SQL injection, IDL-style default float formatting (leading spaces, possible scientific notation) can also produce malformed SQL.

**Suggested Fix:**
```markdown
Use parameterized/prepared statements if XDL supports them; otherwise format explicitly (e.g., STRING(value, FORMAT='(F15.8)')) and never interpolate unvalidated input. Align the example with the 'Use Parameterized Queries' best practice.
```

### 10. [WARNING] security — `docs/CHARTING_FINAL_STATUS.md:96`

*Flagged by: Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud), Ollama (kimi-k2.6:cloud), Ollama (muse-spark-open:cloud)*

**Description:**
'tauri.conf.json' sets 'csp=null' and 'withGlobalTauri=true'. With no CSP, any script injected into the webview (e.g., via a compromised CDN) runs unrestricted, and the global Tauri API widens the IPC attack surface for pages that do not need it.

**Suggested Fix:**
```markdown
Set a restrictive CSP (script-src limited to self and the specific CDN origin if the CDN must stay), and disable 'withGlobalTauri' since generated chart pages have no legitimate need for IPC access.
```

### 14. [WARNING] security — `docs/CHARTING_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md:69`

*Flagged by: Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud), Ollama (kimi-k2.6:cloud), Ollama (muse-spark-open:cloud)*

**Description:**
tauri.conf.json sets 'csp=null' while 'withGlobalTauri=true' and the generated HTML loads ECharts from a third-party CDN (line 42). Any CDN compromise or injected content executes in the webview with full Tauri IPC access, including the exposed 'create_chart_window' command — no CSP layer mitigates it.

**Suggested Fix:**
```markdown
Vendor ECharts/echarts-gl locally (or serve via Tauri's asset protocol) and set a restrictive CSP; scope IPC capabilities so only the intended commands are callable.
```

### 62. [WARNING] correctness — `docs/EMBEDDING_GUIDE.md:71`

*Flagged by: Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud), Ollama (muse-spark-open:cloud)*

**Description:**
The Python ctypes example never sets 'lib.xdl_cleanup.argtypes' (nor a restype), so the 64-bit context pointer returned by 'xdl_init' is passed back as a default C int. On 64-bit platforms this either truncates the pointer (causing a crash/corruption in the native library) or raises an OverflowError 'ctypes' ArgumentError, meaning the quick-start example fails as written.

**Suggested Fix:**
```markdown
Declare the signature before use: 'lib.xdl_cleanup.argtypes = [ctypes.c_void_p]' (and optionally 'lib.xdl_cleanup.restype = None'). The example already does this correctly for 'xdl_call_function', so mirror that pattern.
```

**Suggested Fix:**
```markdown
Expose and document a zero-copy/buffer-based API (e.g., accepting ctypes arrays or bytes/NumPy buffers via 'array.ctypes.data_as') and show that in the performance-focused example, or at least note that '.tolist()' copies the data.
```

### 108. [WARNING] correctness — `docs/3D_PLOTTING_COMPLETE.md:188`

*Flagged by: Ollama (kimi-k2.6:cloud), Ollama (gemma4:cloud)*

**Description:**
The documentation advertises 'Blue (low) to red (high)' as a default height-based colouring scheme. 'Blue-red' is a diverging colormap intended for signed data with a meaningful zero. Using it as a generic sequential ramp for unidirectional height data violates the design-system rule that 'picking the wrong family is a correctness error'.

**Suggested Fix:**
```markdown
Switch the default surface colormap to a perceptually uniform sequential map such as 'viridis', reserving 'blue_red' for genuinely diverging data centred on zero.
```

### 136. [INFO] security — `.claude/hooks/post-edit-check.sh:29`

*Flagged by: Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud)*

**Description:**
The realpath fallback does not resolve symlinks, so if realpath is unavailable a symlink inside the repo pointing to a file outside it passes the containment check and its manifest's build scripts still get executed by cargo. Relatedly, realpath output is fully canonized while $repo_root is a logical path, so on symlinked repo paths (e.g. /tmp vs /private/tmp on macOS) the prefix match at line 31 silently skips all checks.

**Suggested Fix:**
```bash
Canonize both sides the same way: compute repo_root with realpath too (falling back to the same cd/pwd logic), and in the fallback branch, resolve $f through readlink -f when available or reject paths whose dirname contains a symlink component.
```

### 137. [INFO] correctness — `.claude/hooks/post-edit-check.sh:71`

*Flagged by: Ollama (glm-5.3-flash:cloud)*

**Description:**
Both the cargo check and tsc branches print failures to stdout but every path exits 0, so a PostToolUse consumer cannot distinguish a passing check from a failing one and the agent may never see the errors, defeating the hook's purpose as a signal.

**Suggested Fix:**
```bash
Capture the check's exit status and, on failure, write the truncated output to stderr and exit 2 (Claude Code's PostToolUse blocking semantics) so the failing check is surfaced to the model; keep exit 0 only for genuine success or skip.
```

The script uses 'set -uo pipefail' without 'errexit'. When 'cargo check' fails, pipefail returns a non-zero code, but the script continues to the next 'case' statement, whose exit status is always 0. The hook therefore silently succeeds even though the build is broken.

## Suggestions (391)

1. **`CHANGELOG.md`**: Keep a single source of truth for roadmap: drop the hand-maintained 'Pending Features' list from the changelog and rely on TODO.md, which line 332 already links.
2. **`CHANGELOG.md`**: Add measurement context (hardware, matrix sizes, CPU baseline, methodology) for the benchmark claims like 'up to 1517x speedup' (line 202), or link to the benchmark script and its raw output so the numbers are reproducible.
3. **`CHANGELOG.md`**: Tone down subjective hype ('Major language feature!', 'Complete implementation!') in favor of neutral descriptions; a changelog reads more professionally and avoids overpromising 'Complete' features that are partially placeholder (e.g., CASE/SWITCH vs. scientific formats).
4. **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: Extract the identical 'Install Linux GUI system libraries' step (appearing four times) into a composite action so the package list cannot drift between jobs when one copy is updated.
5. **`.claude/skills/add-builtin/SKILL.md`**: SKILL.md hard-codes a 754-arm count that will drift with every added built-in; phrase it as 'a ~750-arm match' or derive the count in scripts/check-builtin-parity.sh output instead of stating it in prose.
6. **`.claude/hooks/post-edit-check.sh`**: The tail -5 / tail -12 truncation can drop the failure summary line cargo emits early in its output; keeping the last lines plus grepping for 'error'/'warning' counts would make the hook's signal more actionable.
7. **`README.md`**: Add README statistics (built-in counts, compatibility percentage, supported Python versions) to the existing consistency-check scripts, since these facts drift independently across edits and nothing currently validates them.
8. **`README.md`**: Replace unsupported figures like '10-100x faster than Pandas' and the bare 88% compatibility number with links to benchmark data or a measured compatibility matrix, in keeping with SOUL.md's 'describe what we measured, not what we expect' principle.
9. **`README.md`**: Move changelog-style markers such as 'NEW' and checkmarks out of the timeless feature list into a CHANGELOG.md so the features section does not require cleanup each release.
10. **`SOUL.md`**: Consider cross-linking each principle in SOUL.md to its concrete enforcement mechanism (CI checks, code-style rules, review checklist) so the philosophy maps to verifiable practice.
11. **`design-system/README.md`**: The 34 hard-coded hex literals are tracked as a prose count (23+6+4+1 at line 34). Convert to an automated guard (CI grep/lint) and link to it, so the count and the 'Replace one when you touch its file' rule cannot go stale.
12. **`TODO.md`**: The 'ColorTable::load_table returns grayscale for unknown tables' bug described in rule 3 is acknowledged as current behaviour but does not appear as an actionable item in TODO.md; add it there (or to an issue tracker) so it survives beyond prose motivation.
13. **`design-system/README.md`**: The README admits the Rust colour constants and tokens.css are two copies of one palette that can drift (lines 117-121). Consider generating the Rust constants from tokens.css (or validating both from a shared source) so the duplication is checked rather than enforced by memory.
14. **`docs/ADVANCED_VIZ_REFERENCE.md`**: Unify code-fence language tags: all XDL snippets are labeled ```idl; pick one consistent tag (e.g., 'xdl') across all documentation so syntax highlighting and tooling stay coherent.
15. **`docs/ADVANCED_VIZ_REFERENCE.md`**: Run instructions differ across docs (one says cd examples and run 'xdl-gui advanced_viz_demo.xdl', another says run from repo root with ./target/release/xdl-gui examples/...). Standardize a single 'How to run' section and cross-link it.
16. **`docs/ADVANCED_VIZ_INTEGRATION.md`**: The integration doc declares 'Integration Complete' in its status footer while the verification checklist still has unchecked TODOs (GUI testing, image display verification). Consider labeling the doc as 'ready for testing' consistently or moving unfinished items to an issue tracker.
17. **`docs/ARRAY_FEATURES.md`**: Add a 'Last Updated' date header to ARRAY_FEATURES.md (like ARRAY_GENERATION_FUNCTIONS.md has) so readers can judge how stale sections such as Known Limitations are.
18. **`docs/ARRAY_STATS_FUNCTIONS.md`**: Establish a single source of truth for feature status (e.g., an IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md that these three docs reference) - per-file status lists are already drifting apart.
19. **`docs/ARRAY_GENERATION_FUNCTIONS.md`**: Use full paths for referenced test files (e.g., 'examples/test_all_indgen.xdl') consistent with the paths used in the other docs, so they can actually be located and run.
20. **`docs/ARRAY_GENERATION_FUNCTIONS.md`**: Tie unverifiable claims like 'All 16 tests passed' and 'Clean build with no warnings' to a commit hash or CI run so they remain auditable as the code changes.
21. **`xdl-charts/src/templates.rs`**: Vendor ECharts locally, set a restrictive CSP, and remove 'withGlobalTauri' - this converts the viewer from 'renders remote code with no policy' to a self-contained offline-capable app.
22. **`xdl-stdlib/src/charting_procs.rs`**: Switch the proc-to-viewer handoff to a randomized, exclusively-created temp file (or a custom URI scheme) and clean it up after spawn.
23. Add a lightweight CI job: build the workspace, run 'xdl-chart-viewer --help-only', run the charting procs against synthetic data headlessly, and validate the example scripts parse.
24. **`docs/CHARTING_FINAL_STATUS.md`**: Consolidate the three overlapping charting docs (INVESTIGATION, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS, FINAL_STATUS) into one living document with a short decision log, so they cannot drift apart as this doc already nearly did.
25. **`docs/CHARTING_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`**: Execute the cleanup pass immediately: delete the two broken scratch files and decide the fate of the unused 'xdl-desktop-viewer' crate (which still pulls Tauri into every workspace build) to cut build time and confusion.
26. **`docs/CHARTING_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`**: Implement the viewer-resolution fallbacks (PATH search, 'XDL_CHART_VIEWER' env var, target-profile sibling) with a loud, actionable error message — this removes the most likely silent no-chart outcome.
27. **`docs/CHARTING_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`**: Add a size guard for heatmap/contour matrices: converting an N x M matrix to [x, y, value] triples can produce multi-megabyte HTML and very slow renders for large inputs; consider downsampling or an arity/size error.
28. **`docs/CHARTING_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`**: Wire the 'keywords' HashMap from 'call_procedure_with_keywords' into the charting procs so TITLE=, XLABEL=, YLABEL=, XRANGE=, YRANGE= work uniformly, replacing positional-only title passing.
29. **`docs/DATABASE_QUICK_START.md`**: Standardize the resource-cleanup pattern across all examples (Connect/Disconnect, rs->Close, OBJ_DESTROY / DF_DROP) and consider showing an error-safe idiom that guarantees cleanup on failure.
30. **`docs/DATAFRAMES_REFERENCE.md`**: Add a short section documenting DataFrame lifetime/ownership rules: every function that returns a new DataFrame ID allocates memory that must later be freed with DF_DROP.
31. **`docs/DATABASE_QUICK_START.md`**: Consider adding a CI check or test harness that executes the documented snippets against a real backend (e.g., in-memory DuckDB/SQLite) to catch drift between docs and implementation.
32. **`docs/DATABASE_QUICK_START.md`**: The batch-insert example (100 individual INSERTs) would benefit from a note recommending multi-row inserts or transactions for performance.
33. **`docs/ECHARTS_INTEGRATION_COMPLETE.md`**: Consolidate the six overlapping charting status documents (CHARTING_WEBGL_INVESTIGATION, CHARTING_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS, CHARTING_FINAL_STATUS, PLOT_SURFACE_ECHARTS_INTEGRATION, TAURI_SUCCESS, and this file) into one living document; mark the superseded ones with a banner pointing to the current one.
34. **`docs/ECHARTS_INTEGRATION_COMPLETE.md`**: Adopt neutral, non-celebratory names for status documents (avoid COMPLETE/SUCCESS-style filenames) and lead with an explicit status line so headlines cannot outlive the facts.

38. **`docs/EMBEDDING_GUIDE.md`**: Add CI checks that validate the guide's snippets (至少 syntax-check the C/Go/Java/Python examples or render/link-check the Markdown), since several code blocks fail as written and will drift as the FFI evolves.
39. **`docs/EMBEDDING_GUIDE.md`**: In the Python quick start, use 'ctypes.util.find_library' or a platform-specific name resolver instead of a hardcoded './libxdl_ffi.dylib', and show how to handle the three possible library filenames per platform.

41. **`docs/EXPLORATION_SUMMARY.md`**: Consider replacing lazy_static with once_cell::sync::Lazy or std::sync::LazyLock for the global registry, and consider tokio-aware locking (RwLock) if the registry is heavily accessed from async contexts.
42. **`docs/FINDGEN_UPDATE.md`**: Document a plan to reconcile the dual array representations: returning XdlValue::Array for 1D and XdlValue::MultiDimArray otherwise forces every consumer to handle two variants; consider normalizing internally (or documenting explicit conversion helpers).

45. **`docs/FUNCTION_REFERENCE.md`**: The claim 'Total Functions: 500+' on line 5 cannot be verified from this document, which enumerates roughly 200 rows and defers the rest to other files; consider linking to a generated inventory or stating counts per section.
46. **`docs/FUNCTION_REFERENCE.md`**: Consider adding a short security note near SPAWN, FILE_DELETE, and FILE_MOVE warning against executing or deleting based on untrusted input, since these are the most dangerous operations documented.
